E1 · Two-stage factorial canvassing field experiment

Class 1 · The article’s primary causal study assigns wards to a 2×2 canvassing status and four of five sampled households within treated wards to treatment, then follows the original assignment through election and October outcomes.

Experiment design: cluster-randomized experiment; factorial experiment

CategoryCriteria met / applicableN/A
Experimental design1/50
Statistical methods2/80
Results1/50
Validity2/20
Design map

Target population

Households in sampled wards in Lahore, Pakistan.
2,500 householdsPDF page 8 · Sample and Timeline

Households are randomly drawn from 500 Lahore wards.

Eligibility criteria

NR
PDF page 8 · Sample and Timeline

Household/respondent eligibility criteria are not stated in the main article; Appendix A.2 is only a pointer.

Recruitment procedure

Random draw of five households per each of 500 wards; contact/consent details otherwise NR.
randomly drawn from 500 wardsPDF page 8 · Sample and Timeline

The sampling frame and recruitment implementation are not supplied.

Field dates and setting

Lahore; baseline June 2018; intervention July before July 25 election; turnout July 26–27; endline October 2018.
baseline survey in June 2018PDF page 8 · Sample and Timeline

The main article supplies the city and major waves/dates.

Number recruited and analyzed

2,500 households/5,000 baseline respondents; turnout N=2,149; autonomy N=2,298; other focal Ns NR.
N 2,149PDF page 13 · Table 5; Table 7

Counts appear across pp. 8, 10–11 and Tables 5/7.

Experimental arms or randomized attributes

2×2: T1 women canvassed; T2 men canvassed; T1+T2 both in separate visits; no-visit control; plus within-treatment untreated households.
2 × 2 factorialPDF page 8 · Random Assignment; Table 3

All four ward statuses and within-ward untreated households are shown.

Unit of randomization

Ward at first stage; household treatment within ward at second stage.
primary randomization unit ... wardPDF page 8 · Random Assignment

Wards receive status, then four of five households receive the ward treatment.

Allocation probabilities and restrictions

Blocked by union council; 125 wards per factorial status; four of five households treated within treatment-status wards, one untreated.
four out of fivePDF page 8 · Random Assignment; Table 3

Table 3 confirms the allocation counts.

Exact treatment and comparator

Gender-matched nonpartisan canvasser; 5-minute video, leaflets, ballot demonstration; exact scripts/materials NR; comparator no visit.
5-minute videoPDF page 7 · Intervention description

Core components are described, with exact component details deferred to appendices.

Outcome definitions and timing

Primary women’s household turnout verified by thumb ink July 26–27; October sticker behavior and six self-report indices; autonomy vignette; full index items/coding NR.
primary outcome measurePDF page 10 · Outcome Data; pp. 11, 15–17

Turnout is fully defined; secondary index items are deferred to supplementary materials.

Estimand

Household-level ITT for separate treatment categories, pooled T1/T2 factors, and interaction; downstream ITTs.
intent-to-treatPDF page 12 · Women’s Turnout

The causal contrasts are named and equations supplied for turnout.

Estimator or statistical model

OLS for turnout and survey indices; differences/DiD for stickers; multinomial logit for autonomy; some implementations NR.
ordinary least squaresPDF page 13 · Tables 5–7; Figure 4

Tables 5–7 and Figure 4 identify model families, with secondary detail gaps.

Analysis unit

Household for turnout; respondents for October survey/sticker/autonomy outcomes.
household levelPDF page 13 · Table 5 note; pp. 15–17

Units vary by outcome and are identifiable.

Variable coding and reference categories

Treatment indicators shown; control omitted; autonomy base; turnout proportion coding explicit; index coding NR.
base = autonomousPDF page 17 · Tables 5 and 7

Reference levels are visible for main displayed models but not all secondary outcomes.

Covariates and fixed effects

Union-council fixed effects; within-treatment-control and individual-randomization indicators; Table 5 unadjusted; appendix-adjusted covariates not in scope.
block ... fixed effectsPDF page 13 · Table 5/Figure 4 notes

Table notes specify included adjustments.

Standard-error and inference procedure

Ward-clustered SEs; Figure 4 90%/95% intervals; multiplicity adjustments described but appendix-only; CI construction NR.
clustered at the ward levelPDF page 13 · Table 5 note; Figure 4 note; p. 16

Inference statements appear in display notes and p. 16.

Handling of clustering or repeated observations

Ward clustering; male/female outcomes separated in Figure 4; full repeated-observation handling NR.
clustered at the ward levelPDF page 13 · Table 5 note

Ward clustering is explicit.

Missing-data and attrition rules

86% turnout verification; 97% household endline recontact; differential attrition and Lee bounds described; complete rules/adjustments NR.
86%PDF page 10 · Turnout; p. 14

Rates and appendix checks are described, not full main-article rules/results.

Effect estimates and uncertainty

Table 5 separate-arm effects: 0.012 (0.028), 0.054 (0.031), 0.080 (0.032); other estimates in Tables 6–7/Figure 4.
0.054PDF page 13 · Table 5; Tables 6–7; Figures 3–4

Table 5 supplies coefficient and clustered SE; Tables 6–7 and figures cover later outcomes.

E2 · Cross-randomized democracy-sticker offer

Class 2 · A new randomized manipulation assigns recontacted male respondents within every original treatment condition to one of two sticker messages and receives a formal difference and difference-in-differences analysis.

Experiment design: completely randomized experiment; survey experiment

CategoryCriteria met / applicableN/A
Experimental design2/50
Statistical methods6/80
Results1/50
Validity1/20
Design map

Target population

Male respondents in original study households recontacted at October endline.
Half ... in each treatment conditionPDF page 10 · Men’s Lasting Support; Figure 1

Male respondents are randomized 1:1 within each original treatment condition.

Eligibility criteria

Male endline respondent; further criteria NR.
Half ... in each treatment conditionPDF page 10 · Men’s Lasting Support; Figure 1

Male respondents are randomized 1:1 within each original treatment condition.

Recruitment procedure

Original household baseline sample recontacted; 97% household recontact overall.
Half ... in each treatment conditionPDF page 10 · Men’s Lasting Support; Figure 1

Male respondents are randomized 1:1 within each original treatment condition.

Field dates and setting

October 2018 endline, respondents’ residences in Lahore.
Half ... in each treatment conditionPDF page 10 · Men’s Lasting Support; Figure 1

Male respondents are randomized 1:1 within each original treatment condition.

Number recruited and analyzed

NR for sticker randomization/analysis.
Half ... in each treatment conditionPDF page 10 · Men’s Lasting Support; Figure 1

Male respondents are randomized 1:1 within each original treatment condition.

Experimental arms or randomized attributes

Generic-democracy sticker versus women-in-democracy sticker.
Half ... in each treatment conditionPDF page 10 · Men’s Lasting Support; Figure 1

Male respondents are randomized 1:1 within each original treatment condition.

Unit of randomization

Male respondent.
Half ... in each treatment conditionPDF page 10 · Men’s Lasting Support; Figure 1

Male respondents are randomized 1:1 within each original treatment condition.

Allocation probabilities and restrictions

One-half per sticker within each original treatment condition.
Half ... in each treatment conditionPDF page 10 · Men’s Lasting Support; Figure 1

Male respondents are randomized 1:1 within each original treatment condition.

E4 · Randomized autonomy-vignette presentation order

Class 3 · The order of three survey vignettes is randomized only to diagnose/prevent order-induced response bias; no order-effect analysis is reported.

Experiment design: completely randomized experiment; survey experiment
